QuickDeliveryModal already computed tier price client-side, so the UI displays the same total the server computes — only the manipulation case is blocked. #15 piece: both `create_quick_delivery` and `convert_quote_to_order` now write `financial_audit_log` entries (operation_type `order_created`/`delivery_created` for QD, `quote_converted` for the conversion). The `financial_audit_log_operation_type_check` constraint was extended to allow the three new tokens. |
| 308 | 20260511100000 | **Phase 1.D — Harden apply_prepay_to_invoice (closes audit Critical #4)** — The function was SECURITY DEFINER and wrote to `prepay_applications`, `prepay_credits`, `customers.prepay_balance_cents`, `invoices.prepay_applied_cents`, and `financial_audit_log` — but had NO actor check, NO role gate, and NO idempotency parameter. Direct PostgREST caller with any authenticated JWT could apply prepay to any invoice, or double-apply on network retry. Practical attack surface was small (only called from `batch_apply_prepayments` which already has canonical idempotency), but defense-in-depth: extends signature to `(p_prepay_credit_id, p_invoice_id, p_amount_cents, p_performed_by uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_idempotency_key text DEFAULT NULL)`. Existing 3-arg callers (`batch_apply_prepayments`) still work because defaults are NULL. Adds `v_actor := auth.uid()` + `AUTH_REQUIRED` check, strict actor pattern (`ACTOR_MISMATCH` if `p_performed_by IS DISTINCT FROM v_actor`), role gate (admin or sales_rep, `INSUFFICIENT_ROLE`), and canonical `check_idempotency`/`save_idempotency` pair. RETURN type stays `uuid` to avoid cascading changes through `batch_apply_prepayments`. DO-block verification asserts overload count = 1, new params present, and all four token-error codes plus both idempotency helpers are in the body. |
| 307 | 20260511090000 | **Phase 1.4 — Profile role-lock trigger (closes audit Critical #3)** — `profiles_update` RLS allowed self-update via `((auth.uid() = id) OR is_admin())` with no column-level restriction, so any authenticated user could PATCH their own row and set `role = 'admin'`, flip `is_active`, or clear `denied_pages`. Adds `_guard_profile_role_lock()` (SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`) wired as `trg_guard_profile_role_lock BEFORE UPDATE ON profiles FOR EACH ROW`. Trigger rejects with `PROFILE_ROLE_LOCK` (errcode `42501`) if `OLD.role IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.role` OR `OLD.is_active IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.is_active` OR `OLD.denied_pages IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.denied_pages` AND `NOT is_admin()`. Non-privilege self-updates (full_name, phone, applicator_license_number, faa_certificate_number) remain allowed. The first half of the privilege-escalation chain (Critical #2 — `handle_new_user` trusting `raw_user_meta_data->>'role'`) stays mitigated by Mason disabling public signup; this trigger holds even if signup is re-enabled. SettingsPage already uses `admin_update_profile` RPC (admin-only, SECURITY DEFINER) so the admin UI is unaffected. DO-block verification asserts function created with correct attributes and trigger active. |
| 302 | 20260511030000 | **Codex review fix for PR #59 — restore positive-total guard on `create_vendor_bill` + add to `update_vendor_bill`** — Two P2 findings from codex review (2026-05-11). (1) `update_vendor_bill` (migration 20260510100000) validated `p_subtotal_cents > 0` but never re-checked the computed `v_new_total_cents`; a $100 bill edited with a -$200 adjustment yielded `total_cents = -10000`, `balance_cents = -10000` (GENERATED), `status = 'unpaid'` — broken aging/payment behavior. (2) `create_vendor_bill` rewrite in `ap_polish_completion` (20260510130000) silently dropped the `v_total <= 0` guard that codex audit F4 had added in PR-04 (20260510030000); negative adjustment could flip total negative. Both functions now `RAISE EXCEPTION 'INVALID_AMOUNT: bill total must be positive (got %)', v_total` after computing total = subtotal + adjustment. Bodies otherwise reproduced verbatim from the prior installed migrations (preserving PR-22b PO consistency check + drift soft-warn in `create_vendor_bill`; preserving update audit log + active-payments guard in `update_vendor_bill`). DO-block verification asserts both guards landed and that the PR-22b polish features (VENDOR_PO_MISMATCH, vendor_bill_drift) were not regressed. No frontend changes — existing handlers already display `INVALID_AMOUNT` errors raised for the subtotal check. |

| 251 | 20260430190000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 7 — Codex re-review #1+#2+#3 hot fixes** — three bugs surfaced by the codex re-review of Phases 1–6. **#1 (P1)** added internal auth gates to `start_job` + `complete_job`: validate `auth.uid() = p_performed_by`, then check role (`is_admin()` OR `is_sales_rep()` OR (`is_applicator()` AND `v_job.applicator_id = auth.uid()`)). Closes the privilege-escalation hole that Phase 5's RLS could not (SECURITY DEFINER bypasses RLS). **#2 (P1)** changed `complete_job`'s linked-prebook lookup from `inventory_holds.source_id = jobs.quote_section_id` to `... = jobs.quote_id` — planned-program holds are keyed by quote_id (verified against migration `20260317100000:384,397`); the previous lookup never matched. **#3 (P2)** replaced the single-row hold UPDATE with an oldest-first loop that takes up to each row's `quantity`, preventing CHECK violations when multiple holds exist for the same quote+product. |
| 252 | 20260430200000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 8 — Codex re-review #4 hot fix** — `save_field_app_invoice` edit path now cancels and detaches orphan group child invoices when an admin edits a draft grouped invoice and the new derived customer list drops a previously-billed customer. Per Mason's call (Option B), orphans are marked `status = cancelled`, `invoice_group_id = NULL`, totals zeroed (consistent with the items/shares already wiped), and an `invoice_orphan_cancelled` activity_feed row records the audit trail. Posted/voided members remain protected by the existing edit lock. |
| 253 | 20260430210000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 9 — Sprint A1 auth gates on 3 field-app billing RPCs** — addresses P1 findings from money-inventory and security-permissions audits (2026-04-30). Pattern mirrors `save_quote` and Phase 7 `start_job`/`complete_job`. SECURITY DEFINER bypasses RLS, so internal auth checks are the only protection — previously these RPCs could be called by any authenticated user with a spoofed `p_performed_by`/`p_created_by`. |
| 254 | 20260430220000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 10 — Sprint A2 + Sprint B (folded)** — auth gates + invoice integrity rules on `save_invoice` and `create_invoice_from_order`. Both RPCs use `auth.uid()` directly inline (no `p_performed_by`), so the fix is admin/sales role check + business-rule validation. **`save_invoice`**: now reads `order_id`/`blend_ticket_id` from the `p_invoice` payload and **rejects** standalone-create attempts that link to neither (CLAUDE.md hard rule). **`create_invoice_from_order`**: now **rejects** duplicate active invoices for the same order (any non-cancelled/non-voided invoice counts), preventing the click-twice overbilling bug. Frontend: removed the orphan "New Invoice" buttons on `Invoices.tsx` (top action bar + empty-state CTA) since they pointed to a path that violates the hard rule; users now create invoices via Order Detail or Blend Ticket Detail. |
| 255 | 20260430230000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 11 — Sprint C: field-app RLS lockdown** — closes 2 P1 findings from `2026-04-30-security-permissions-audit-findings.md`. `field_app_locations` and `field_app_location_shares` previously had `USING (true) / WITH CHECK (true)` on every operation, allowing any authenticated user to read AND mutate these tables directly via PostgREST and bypass `save_field_app_invoice` entirely. Tightened: SELECT remains broad (parent invoice/job RLS still applies), INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE restricted to admin/sales. `application_records.app_records_select` now scopes applicators to `applicator_id = auth.uid()` so they can only see their own assigned application history. |
| 256 | 20260430240000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 12 — Sprint A3 + part of D** — auth gates on `confirm_delivery`, `complete_delivery`, `create_quick_delivery`. Replaced `v_actor := COALESCE(p_performed_by, auth.uid())` with strict pattern: reject if not authenticated, reject if `p_performed_by IS NOT NULL AND IS DISTINCT FROM auth.uid()`. Each function's existing role check preserved. **Folded fix from Sprint D**: `complete_delivery` now requires `status='in_progress'` (was: only rejected `completed`/`cancelled`, allowed completing directly from `scheduled` and skipping the start step). 3 of 5 remaining P1 actor-spoofing vectors closed. |

This migration adds a due_date check after the lift: `'paid' && balance > 0 && due_date < CURRENT_DATE` -> `'overdue'`, otherwise `'posted'`. Each policy was dropped and recreated with the same action, roles, permissive flag, and predicate — only the auth.uid() calls changed. The 8 remaining flagged policies overlapped with `multiple_permissive_policies` and were handled in migration 287 (which consolidates AND wraps in one go). | 287 | 20260511060000 | **Perf sweep #2 — consolidate overlapping permissive RLS policies** (Supabase advisor `multiple_permissive_policies`, 33 WARN → 23 unique overlap groups across 16 tables). Multiple permissive policies covering the same (role, action) cause Postgres to evaluate each predicate per row and OR them together. Consolidation merges each overlap group into one permissive policy whose predicate is the OR of the originals — same union of access, one evaluation per row instead of N. Patterns handled: (A) `_admin_all FOR ALL` + role-specific INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/SELECT (`deliveries`, `delivery_items`, `delivery_photos`, `delivery_remainders`, `receiving_photos`, `receiving_records`) — split the FOR ALL into action-specific policies merged with the role policies; (B) `_admin_all FOR ALL` + open `_select` qual=true (`application_services`, `customer_application_rates`, `quote_pdf_templates`, `quote_templates`) — drop the admin_all SELECT side (the open SELECT covers it), keep admin INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE narrow; (C) admin SELECT + sales_rep SELECT (`purchase_orders`, `purchase_order_items`) — merge with `is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()`; (D) admin SELECT + user-own SELECT (`document_processing_log`, `commissions`) — merge with admin OR ownership predicate; (E) redundant admin policies (`failed_notifications` had a SELECT policy redundant with a FOR ALL one — drop the SELECT). **Sensitive-table change:** `rate_limit_log` had a permissive `qual=false` "Deny all direct access" policy that was a no-op (permissive false OR'd with permissive admin = admin; access semantics unchanged). Replaced with a **RESTRICTIVE** `FOR ALL admin_only` policy as defense-in-depth — if a future migration ever adds a permissive policy by mistake, the RESTRICTIVE still blocks non-admins. All rewritten policies also use `(SELECT auth.uid())` wrapping so this migration closes the 8 residual auth_rls_initplan findings. Verification asserts old policies are gone, 39 new policies exist, and the RESTRICTIVE policy is the correct permissive flag. |

File: `20260810151000_whole_cent_money_check_constraints.sql`. The hard-guard answer to CodeRabbit's "use bigint cents" Major finding on PR #354, which is being closed **won't-fix with a hard guard substituted** rather than dismissed (rationale: `docs/audits/2026-08-10-order-profit-bigint-cents-evaluation.md`). Adds `CHECK (col IS NULL OR (col = ROUND(col,2) AND col > '-Infinity'::numeric AND col < 'Infinity'::numeric))` to the **7 money columns measured 100% clean**: `orders.total_cost`, `orders.total_profit`, `order_items.profit`, `quotes.total_price`, `quotes.total_profit`, `quote_items.total_price`, `quote_items.profit`. This gives at type level what bigint cents would give — sub-cent and non-finite both unrepresentable — with zero reader or writer changes, because the stored unit stays dollars. **BOTH halves of the predicate are load-bearing.** PostgreSQL `numeric` does not use IEEE NaN semantics: so numeric can be sorted and tree-indexed, it treats NaN as equal to NaN and greater than every non-NaN value, so `'NaN' = ROUND('NaN',2)` is TRUE and the ROUND clause lets NaN through; `'NaN' < 'Infinity'` is FALSE and is what actually rejects it. Do not "simplify" this to one clause. **5 columns are deliberately deferred and the migration asserts they are NOT constrained** so nobody can quietly widen it: `orders.total_price`, `order_items.total_price` (35/288 dirty), `quotes.total_cost` (2/4), `commissions.commission_amount` and `commissions.order_profit` (3/35 each). `NOT VALID` was considered and rejected: it only skips the initial backfill scan, and a CHECK still re-evaluates the whole new row on every UPDATE, so shipping it over dirty rows would freeze those records against all future edits. Repairing the 43 dirty rows rewrites stored money and is its own migration needing separate approval; the remaining constraints follow it as VALID. **Must apply after rows 868 and 869** — DELTA-E and DELTA-B2 exist precisely to keep the quote→order conversion and the quote save from hard-failing against these constraints; filename stamps enforce the order. Counts are the state *after* live migration `20260810025159_backfill_stale_line_profit` (from unmerged branch `claude/session-orchestration-setup-d73e6c`); pre-backfill it was 46 dirty `order_items.total_price`, not 35. Residual path noted in-file: `restore_quote_version` replays `quote_versions.snapshot_data` verbatim into 4 constrained columns without rounding — all 3 live snapshots measured clean on 2026-08-10, so no restore can fail today. **Post-apply live verification (2026-08-10):** applied third and last. `pg_constraint` re-read live: exactly the intended **7** constraints exist, each `contype = 'c'`, each `convalidated = true`, each on its intended table, and `pg_get_constraintdef` reproduces both halves of the predicate. A pattern query for every `*_whole_cents_chk` in the `public` schema returns those 7 and nothing else, so all 5 deferred columns are confirmed still unconstrained. (**Superseded as live state, read-only re-check 2026-08-19 UTC:** `20260812115238_repair_historical_order_line_cents` later repaired `order_items.total_price` and added its own validated CHECK in the same migration, so live now returns **8** `*_whole_cents_chk` constraints and **4** unconstrained deferred columns. This row stays an accurate record of what migration 870 itself did; the current enforced/deferred lists live in `docs/manual/DECISION_LOG.md`.) The in-migration probe block ran at apply time and passed, which is what proves the predicate bites: it rejects a sub-cent value, NaN, `Infinity` and `-Infinity`, and accepts a whole-cent value. The NaN semantics were re-checked against live Postgres by direct evaluation: `10.005 = round(10.005,2)` is FALSE, `'NaN' = round('NaN',2)` is **TRUE** (the ROUND clause alone would let NaN through), and `'NaN' < 'Infinity'` is FALSE (the bounds clause is what rejects it). **No live row was modified by any of the three migrations.** |

This row previously read "NOT APPLIED LIVE — pending a clean adversarial proof and merge of PR #371", which was true when written and was corrected on 2026-08-12 against `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`. File: `20260811200000_blend_ticket_order_whole_cent_totals.sql`. Fixes the one deterministic regression the exact-SHA `gpt-5.6-sol` review raised against row 870: the validated whole-cent CHECKs that row added to `orders.total_cost` and `orders.total_profit` are violated by `create_order_from_blend_ticket`, which ended by overwriting the order header with raw unrounded price x converted-quantity sums. Any blend ticket whose unit conversion yields a fractional quantity would have had its entire completed-ticket to order transaction rolled back. **The defect is latent, not historical** — production holds no blend ticket that has produced an order, so no order was ever lost. `CREATE OR REPLACE`s the function, re-emitted from the already-applied `20260714230200_blend_ticket_order_lifecycle.sql` whose body was md5-matched against the live `prosrc` immediately before finalising this file (both `19e08adc9d62e13b7482210f6167734d`, 10,064 bytes, once CRLF line endings are preserved on the disk side) — confirming no live drift for this function, with exactly two deltas: the raw header `UPDATE orders SET total_price = ... total_margin_pct = ...` is deleted and replaced by a read-back of the header the AFTER-ROW trigger `after_order_items_change` (`trg_recalc_order_totals`) has already written from the per-line values, and the two hand-maintained running totals that fed only that removed statement are dropped as dead code along with the now-unused `v_total_cost` declaration. Per-line money, actor binding, idempotency, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path` and grants are byte-identical to the applied source. **Scope was deliberately narrowed after live verification.** An earlier draft also rounded the per-line `total_price`, `profit` and `net_margin` in the `order_items` INSERT. Live `pg_constraint` shows no whole-cent CHECK on `order_items.total_price` or `net_margin`, and the BEFORE-ROW trigger `trg_order_items_round_money` (`_round_money_to_whole_cents`) *derives* `order_items.profit` from pre-rounded components and discards whatever the caller passes, so the per-line INSERT could never have violated a constraint and those three changes were removed as redundant scope. Because the header is now written only by the trigger, a missing or disabled trigger would silently leave every blend-ticket order header at the literal zeros the `orders` INSERT seeds rather than raising, so the migration ends with a fail-closed `DO` block asserting exactly one overload of the function, that `after_order_items_change` exists on `public.order_items`, is neither disabled nor replica-only, runs `trg_recalc_order_totals`, and fires on INSERT; that the removed raw-overwrite text is absent from the new `prosrc` while the replacement read-back is present; and that the actor-binding and idempotency markers survived the re-emission. Identifying the trigger by the function it runs and the events it fires on, rather than by name alone, closes the case where a same-named trigger has been repointed. The read-back inside the function also now raises `ORDER_HEADER_UNREADABLE` instead of returning a NULL total if the just-inserted order row is ever unreadable. Live preflight at authoring time: ledger high-water `20260811183437`, this version strictly greater and `already_applied = false`. Each postcondition assertion was additionally executed read-only against live before commit and observed to pass (`tgenabled = O`, `tgtype = 29` so the INSERT bit is set, trigger function `public.trg_recalc_order_totals`, and exactly one version of each of the two functions), so the block gates on a real condition rather than false-failing the apply. |
| 872 | 20260813010000 | **PARKED DRAFT (STAGED) — NOT APPLIED.** Moved out of the live replay path to scripts/.staging-migrations/ by PR #393 (2026-08-13) pending the Phase 2 governed apply pipeline; the staged file is pinned byte-for-byte by SQL sha256 `43fe0a8ceaba189d9cae356ab357d95a8e3aeb42f315683567b93468c5b1eb68` (computed 2026-08-13 over the staged bytes, including the PR #393 review fixes). File: `20260813010000_wave_a_order_cost_authority_and_finiteness.sql`, branch `claude/wave-a-money`. Wave A of the 2026-08-09 ordering-cycle review. Two confirmed findings on the `create_direct_order` seam. (1) **Caller-controlled cost drove the commission basis (HIGH).** `create_direct_order` took `unit_cost` from the request payload verbatim, so a rep could submit a low cost, inflate the order profit that `_insert_commissions_for_order` reads, and raise their own commission liability — no API access needed, `NewOrder.tsx` exposes unit cost as an editable field. This migration makes `products.current_cost` authoritative on that path, the same rule `20260806000752_authorize_bulk_import_product_cost` applied to `bulk_import_order`. `convert_quote_to_order` is deliberately **not** touched: the review's own verifier and the independent Codex triage both refuted that half from source, because `save_quote` overwrites every line's cost and profit from `products.current_cost` before conversion. (2) **Non-finite money and quantities were accepted (HIGH after triage).** A NaN quantity passes the `<= 0` skip test (`NaN <= 0` is false) and compares greater than every finite value, so `CHECK (quantity_prebooked >= 0)` passes too — the value reaches `inventory` and poisons Net Position for every later quote and order of that product. Verified against live `pg_constraint`: the 2026-08-10 whole-cent constraints (row 870) cover `orders.total_cost`, `orders.total_profit` and `order_items.profit`, but **not** `orders.total_price`, `order_items.total_price`, `inventory.quantity_prebooked` or `commissions.commission_amount`. Rejecting at the RPC boundary closes those and replaces an opaque constraint violation with an actionable message. Also corrects two refusal messages from non-canonical prose to the canonical `ACTOR_MISMATCH` / `AUTH_REQUIRED` tokens `src/lib/db.ts` maps to friendly text; a grep over `src/` returned zero matches for the old prose, so no caller or test keyed on it. **No backfill — moves no live money.** |

File: `20260813020000_round_order_header_money.sql`, branch `claude/wave-a-money`. Extends `_round_money_to_whole_cents()` with an `orders` branch and adds `trg_orders_round_money` — `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF total_price, total_cost, total_profit ON public.orders` — so the order header can no longer store sub-cent money by any path. `order_items` and `commissions` already had this protection (rows 860-862); the header did not. **A claim in an earlier draft of this file's header was wrong and was corrected after drift review.** That draft said `trg_recalc_order_totals` already writes `total_price` rounded, making the new trigger inert on the ordinary path. It is not the last writer: `_update_order_items_impl` (live body 13,278 chars, `SECURITY DEFINER`) ends with an un-rounded header write of the raw line sum. Confirmed by reading the live body directly rather than accepting the reviewer's word. Live counts read 2026-08-10 that justify keeping `total_price` in the trigger's scope: 35 `order_items` rows hold a sub-cent `total_price` across 16 distinct orders, **0** order headers hold a sub-cent `total_price`, and 15 of 65 orders already have a header that differs from the exact line sum. Rounding therefore preserves the invariant the data actually holds; narrowing the trigger to exclude `total_price` would newly introduce sub-cent headers. Fail-closed pre-condition pins the live `_round_money_to_whole_cents` body **at both ends**: md5 `17955b2eace3c566b23f506118770f9b` before the change, md5 `71a66b7a69ab6984efa17ec79bc9e4a5` after it. Those two are the only accepted states and anything else refuses. An earlier draft merely detected the already-applied case and carried on with a `NOTICE`, which security review showed to be a silent-clobber path — a later migration adding a fourth branch to the same function would be reverted by a re-run of this file, and every post-condition here checks only the three branches this file knows about, so all of them would still pass. That is the recorded pending-migration overlap-clobber class, so the replay branch is now pinned rather than trusted. Post-conditions assert the trigger is ROW/BEFORE/INSERT/UPDATE and enabled, assert via `pg_trigger.tgattr` that it is column-scoped to exactly `total_cost, total_price, total_profit` (`tgtype` alone cannot see the column list), and run a rollback-only probe covering **both** the INSERT and the UPDATE path, each failing closed if the row is missing. `lock_timeout`/`statement_timeout` are set with plain `SET` and released with `RESET`, not `SET LOCAL` — confirmed empirically that `SET LOCAL` outside a transaction block only emits a warning and is discarded. **No backfill — moves no live money.** |
| 874 | 20260813030000 | **PARKED DRAFT (STAGED) — NOT APPLIED.** Moved out of the live replay path to scripts/.staging-migrations/ by PR #393 (2026-08-13) pending the Phase 2 governed apply pipeline; the staged file is pinned byte-for-byte by SQL sha256 `1880ff1e006645cd1253eabc3ca51595d89687bbe6646e88c2b291b730a203ef` (computed 2026-08-13 over the staged bytes, including the PR #393 review fixes). File: `20260813030000_reject_non_finite_money_and_quantities.sql`, branch `claude/wave-a-money`. The database-level half of row 872: 30 `*_finite_chk` CHECK constraints of the form `CHECK (col IS NULL OR (col > '-Infinity'::numeric AND col < 'Infinity'::numeric))` across `quote_items` (9), `quotes` (2), `orders` (2), `order_items` (10), `inventory` (5) and `commissions` (2). Row 872 rejects non-finite input at one RPC; these constraints close every writer. **The strict `>` and `<` are load-bearing and must not be "simplified" to `>= 0`.** PostgreSQL `numeric` does not use IEEE NaN semantics — it sorts NaN above every finite value so it can be tree-indexed — so an existing `col >= 0` CHECK rejects `-Infinity` but **accepts NaN**. Only the two-sided strict bound rejects all three of NaN, `Infinity` and `-Infinity`. **Live pre-condition evidence (2026-08-10):** all 30 columns queried on production in one pass — **0 non-finite rows in every one**, so each constraint validates without rejecting existing data. Re-runnable by construction **without dropping anything**: PostgreSQL has no `ADD CONSTRAINT IF NOT EXISTS`, and the staged file performs no `DROP CONSTRAINT` at all — for each name it reads any existing same-named constraint back with `pg_get_constraintdef` (matched on table **and** name, so a same-named constraint on another table cannot satisfy the check), leaves an equivalent definition in place, adds the constraint only when missing, and aborts on a conflicting definition instead of replacing it. The structural post-condition does not merely count the constraints — it reads each one back with `pg_get_constraintdef` and fails unless the rendered predicate names the column on both bounds, so a constraint attached to the wrong column cannot pass by name alone. Same plain `SET`/`RESET` timeout treatment as row 873; `lock_timeout` matters more here because `ADD CONSTRAINT` takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE and would otherwise queue behind an open transaction and block readers behind it. **No backfill — moves no live money.** |

File: `20260813040000_clamp_negative_commission_remainder.sql`, branch `claude/wave-a-money`. Wave A fix #3 of the 2026-08-09 ordering-cycle review. Three functions — `_insert_commissions_for_order`, `_insert_commissions_for_job`, and `_save_invoice_scoped_impl` — give the **last** commission-split row the rounding remainder as `GREATEST(ROUND(profit,2),0) - SUM(earlier rows)`. The `GREATEST` clamps the *total*, not the difference, so the remainder itself can go negative: on a valid four-way 25% split of a $0.02 profit the earlier rows round to 0.01 each, sum to 0.03, and the last row lands at -0.01. `commissions.chk_commission_amount CHECK (commission_amount >= 0)` then aborts the whole transaction with a raw check-constraint error, taking down the entire quote conversion / order creation / invoice save. The fix clamps the last row at zero and caps every earlier row at the budget still unspent, so the rows can never sum past the rounded total. The cap is **inert on every split that succeeds today** — if the old remainder was already >= 0, every running total was within budget and `LEAST()` returns the unchanged per-row share. Measured read-only against live across 15 split shapes: exactly the two currently-aborting shapes change, the other 13 are byte-identical, and every case sums exactly to `GREATEST(ROUND(profit,2),0)` with no negative row. Each `CREATE OR REPLACE` is the exact live statement text (md5-verified against `pg_proc.prosrc`) with only the reconciliation `CASE` rewritten. Pre/postconditions were executed against live read-only on 2026-08-10 and both pass: the precondition ran to its final assertion (all three md5 pins, the `compute_commission_amount` non-negativity clamp across every overload, `_save_invoice_scoped_impl` still `SECURITY DEFINER`, `chk_commission_amount` present), and the postcondition's structural half proved non-vacuous — as-is it fails on the missing clamp marker, softened it passes the overload count, all three `prosecdef` flags, the pinned `search_path`, and the anon/authenticated/PUBLIC EXECUTE-grant check. Catalog lookups are by function **name**, not by a `::regprocedure` signature literal, because a signature literal for a re-declared function aborts with a raw cast error before any readable message can fire; uniqueness is asserted separately. **Not applied, not reviewed by Codex, and blocked behind PR #371.** |

File: `20260813050000_guard_job_commission_split_immutable.sql`, branch `claude/wave-a-money`. Wave A fix #4 of the 2026-08-09 ordering-cycle review. `jobs.commission_split` is the jsonb column read at invoicing time to mint commission rows, and today any signed-in sales rep can rewrite it directly. `jobs_update` is `USING (is_admin() OR is_sales_rep())` with no ownership scoping — unlike `quotes_update`, which *is* scoped for reps — the ACL baseline grants table-wide UPDATE on `public.jobs` to `authenticated`, and `_enforce_billed_job_immutability` enumerates 36 columns of which `commission_split` is not one. A rep can therefore re-point a colleague's commission at themselves through the ordinary REST update path, and the money follows at invoicing. Voiding an invoice returns the job to `completed` and re-invoicing re-mints from the column, so a split rewritten on an invoiced job is not inert. This adds `_guard_job_commission_split_immutable()` (SECURITY INVOKER, pinned `search_path`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated/service_role) on a `BEFORE UPDATE OF commission_split` trigger. **Four allowances, in order:** an unchanged rewrite (`IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`) returns immediately; filling a NULL is allowed **only when `current_user` is the owner of `public.jobs`**; a rewrite is allowed while the transaction-local GUC `crx.job_split_correction_authorized` holds **this row's own id**, which only the admin correction RPC described below can set; every other change raises `JOB_COMMISSION_SPLIT_IMMUTABLE`. **The caller check is an allowlist, deliberately** — an earlier draft named the three Supabase API roles and refused them, which fails open for any role nobody thought to list (a future custom role, a Studio SQL-editor session, `dashboard_user`); the shipped form looks the owner up from `pg_class`/`pg_roles` at fire time and refuses everything else. Without a caller check at all, the NULL branch is the original hole restricted to not-yet-invoiced rows, and this guard would then freeze the forgery permanently. Both legitimate writers are `SECURITY DEFINER` owned by the `jobs` table owner (verified live 2026-08-11 **and asserted in the precondition**, because `CREATE OR REPLACE` silently resets a function to SECURITY INVOKER when the clause is omitted and `transfer_job_to_invoice` has been re-emitted several times), so `current_user` is that owner inside them while a REST update runs as an API role; `current_user` is not client-settable, so this is caller identity, not a bypass flag. **Which jobs actually carry a value — corrected from the first draft.** A NULL split is not a rare legacy leftover: `jobs_snapshot_commission_split` fills the column on INSERT only when `quote_id IS NULL`, so every quote-derived job — the primary scheduling path — is inserted NULL and stays NULL until invoicing. The freeze therefore bites at invoicing for those jobs, and the value it freezes comes from the parent quote's split or the customer default, which makes the real control surface there the ownership scoping on `save_quote`/`save_customer`; this guard makes an upstream mistake permanent rather than preventing it. **Trigger order is load-bearing.** Same-timing triggers fire alphabetically by `tgname` (type `name`, C collation), and `trg_guard_…` sorts ahead of `trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids`, so the guard sees the caller's raw NEW rather than the stamper's name→id enrichment. **That ordering is now proved from the catalog, not from a literal.** The first draft compared two string constants, which is a tautology that cannot fail; the shipped precondition queries `pg_trigger`/`pg_proc` for every BEFORE-ROW-UPDATE trigger on `public.jobs` whose function assigns `NEW.commission_split` — matching **both** `NEW.commission_split :=` and `… INTO NEW.commission_split`, since `jobs_snapshot_commission_split` uses both forms — and aborts unless each one sorts strictly after the guard. **Other assertions added after review:** the migration must be applied by the `jobs` table owner (the probe's owner-fill would otherwise abort on a false alarm); `_guard_recipient_name_reuse` must be installed and not disabled, because the unchanged-rewrite allowance delegates to the stamper and is only safe while that guard forbids acquiring a referenced profile name; `save_job` must not assign the column, so ordinary job saves never reach this guard; no `public` function may use a SQL-standard (`BEGIN ATOMIC`) body, which lives in `prosqlbody` and is invisible to every `prosrc` regex in the file; and the writer-detection regex is anchored on the `SET` clause (`UPDATE … SET … commission_split =`, tolerating `UPDATE ONLY` and the `%I`/`%s` of dynamic SQL) so a statement that merely reads the column in a `WHERE` clause is not miscounted as a writer. **The one way through is an audited admin correction (Mason's decision, 2026-08-11).** The first draft exposed no escape hatch at all, which meant a wrong split could only be fixed by writing a migration; Mason chose "let admins correct it, with a record" instead. This migration therefore also creates `public.correct_job_commission_split(p_job_id uuid, p_new_split jsonb, p_reason text, p_performed_by uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_idempotency_key text DEFAULT NULL)` — `SECURITY DEFINER`, pinned `search_path`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC/anon/service_role and granted to `authenticated` only. It requires `auth.uid()`, refuses an `p_performed_by` that disagrees with it, requires `is_admin()`, requires a non-blank reason, revalidates the new value through the existing `validate_commission_split_json`, takes `FOR UPDATE` on the job, returns early and unchanged when the value already matches, and writes a `financial_audit_log` row (`operation_type = 'split_modified'`, `entity_type = 'split'`) recording the old and new values plus the reason. **Three properties make the handshake honest rather than a bypass flag.** The GUC carries the **job id**, not a boolean, so a value left set for one job cannot authorize an edit to a different one in the same transaction; `set_config(..., true)` is transaction-local and is additionally cleared immediately after the UPDATE; and it is only settable from inside an admin-gated `SECURITY DEFINER` function, since a raw PostgREST row update cannot issue `SET LOCAL` at all. The guard trigger **stays `SECURITY INVOKER`** — making it DEFINER would set `current_user` to the table owner and silently void the NULL-fill caller check — and the postcondition asserts `NOT prosecdef` on it. `SECURITY DEFINER` on the RPC is not a preference: `financial_audit_log` grants INSERT to `postgres` only, so a caller-rights function physically could not write its own audit row. The audit row records the value read back with `RETURNING`, not the value requested, because the stamper enriches recipient ids after the guard runs. **The bulk-repair path is unchanged and still available:** `ALTER TABLE public.jobs DISABLE TRIGGER …` / `ENABLE TRIGGER` inside a reviewed migration — owner-only, unreachable from any Supabase API role, and visible in the diff. Precedent: migration `20260722174029_commission_split_recipient_ids` carried a top-level backfill that this guard would have refused. **What changes for the office:** nobody loses the ability to fix a mistake; they lose the ability to fix one invisibly. A rep can no longer rewrite a split at all, and an admin can, but every correction leaves a dated row naming who changed it, from what, to what, and why. This brings the guard in line with its sibling `_enforce_billed_job_immutability`, which already exempts admins so they can correct billed history. **Live evidence 2026-08-11 (read-only).** The precondition — including all four new assertions — was executed against production byte-identically to the migration's own copy and ran to `PRECOND_VALIDATION_ALL_ASSERTIONS_PASSED`: applier is the table owner, single jsonb column, stamper present, no competing assigning trigger sorts ahead of the guard, `_guard_recipient_name_reuse` live on `profiles` and enabled, `save_job` clean, no SQL-standard bodies anywhere in `public`, the hardened regex still returns exactly two writers (`transfer_job_to_invoice`, `_save_field_app_split_invoice_impl`) both writing through the fill-only `COALESCE(commission_split, …)` pattern, and both `prosecdef` and owned by the `jobs` owner. **The correction path's own preconditions were then verified live, read-only, on 2026-08-11:** all four borrowed dependencies exist (`validate_commission_split_json`, `is_admin`, `check_idempotency`, `save_idempotency`); `financial_audit_log_operation_type_check` already permits `'split_modified'` and `financial_audit_log_entity_type_check` already permits `'split'`, so **no CHECK-constraint widening is needed** and this migration adds none; and production currently has 4 active admins, 1 active sales rep, 3 jobs with a split set and 1 with a NULL split — every row and role shape the probes require, so none of them will fall back to a weaker proof. **One real bug was caught here before it shipped:** `check_idempotency` returns NULL on a NULL key but `save_idempotency` *raises* `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED` on one, so both save calls are wrapped in `IF p_idempotency_key IS NOT NULL`, matching the `complete_team_note` precedent. Without that, every correction made without an idempotency key would have failed **after** rewriting the split. **A probe from the first draft was removed as provably wrong.** It fired an API-role no-op save to prove the REVOKE had not broken browser writes, on the rationale that a trigger fire might be privilege-checked. PostgreSQL checks EXECUTE on a trigger function at `CREATE TRIGGER` time, not each time it fires, so the probe tested nothing; the same conclusion is independently supported by 40 existing production triggers that already have EXECUTE revoked from `authenticated` on tables the browser writes daily, and by the trigger being scoped `UPDATE OF commission_split` on a column `save_job` never writes. **The behavioural probe is exercised at apply time and rolls itself back.** It hard-fails rather than skipping if either row shape is missing, and live currently has both. It proves seven things: an owner rewrite of a set split is refused; a no-op rewrite is allowed; an API-role fill of a NULL is refused, which is the caller check itself; an owner-level fill of a NULL still succeeds, which is the invoicing path; **the GUC is job-scoped, proved by setting it to a different job's id and confirming the rewrite is still refused** — the single most important assertion in the file, because if that ever starts passing the correction path has degraded into a global switch; a non-admin calling the correction RPC is refused with `ADMIN_REQUIRED`, exercised under a real active sales rep's identity via a transaction-local `request.jwt.claims`; and a real active admin's correction succeeds, returns `changed = true`, and leaves **exactly one** new `financial_audit_log` row, with the GUC verified empty on return. That last one hard-fails rather than skipping if live has no active admin: do not install this guard without watching an admin correction actually succeed. Both new probes disclose in the header that they call the RPC for real — one split rewritten, one audit row inserted — and both are rolled back with the rest of the probe block. The role-switching probe captures `current_user` up front and restores it with `SET LOCAL ROLE %I` rather than `RESET ROLE`, which would drop to the session login role and run the owner-only probe under the wrong identity, and it asserts the restore actually took effect before that probe runs. The no-op probe re-fires the stamper, so a fail-closed recipient-resolution error is re-raised with a message naming the row and the stamper instead of surfacing as an unexplained guard failure. Residue is proven by hashing every split on the table in id order before and after and requiring the hashes to match — the sentinel-string check it replaced was vacuous, since the sentinel only ever appears in writes the guard refuses. The overload check is documented as a tripwire rather than a proof: a zero-argument signature cannot be overloaded under `CREATE OR REPLACE`, so it exists to catch a future edit that adds a parameter. **Not applied, not reviewed by Codex, and blocked behind PR #371.** |
| 877 | 20260813060000 | **PARKED DRAFT (STAGED) — NOT APPLIED.** Moved out of the live replay path to scripts/.staging-migrations/ by PR #393 (2026-08-13) pending the Phase 2 governed apply pipeline; the staged file is pinned byte-for-byte by SQL sha256 `23f8ac26c6d18a6b1d609bad531dbb593a1679de33aa39ac3821fad1765c0a3a` (computed 2026-08-13 over the staged bytes, including the PR #393 review fixes). File: `20260813060000_require_completed_delivery_before_invoice_post.sql`, branch `claude/wave-a-money`. Wave A fix #5 of the 2026-08-09 ordering-cycle review, shape chosen by Mason on 2026-08-11: **an invoice tied to a delivery cannot be posted until that delivery is actually completed.** Today it can. `_create_quick_delivery_intent_impl_20260802` inserts the delivery row with `status = 'scheduled'` and, in the same call, creates the draft invoice carrying that `delivery_id` — so a bill exists for goods still in the warehouse, and posting it succeeds. **Why that is a money bug, not a policy preference.** The draft is created at *ordered* quantity. `_complete_delivery_authorized_impl`'s adjustment loop only rewrites invoices whose status is still `'draft'`, so posting first makes the delivery-time correction skip the invoice and freezes the customer at the ordered quantity even when less was delivered. `trg_snapshot_line_shares_on_post` also freezes line shares at post time, which is why widening the adjustment loop to posted invoices is the wrong repair — gating the post is the right one. **One function covers all three posting paths.** `_post_invoice_impl_20260714(p_invoice_id uuid, p_idempotency_key text DEFAULT NULL)` is the only function that flips `invoices.status` to `'posted'` on a normal path; exactly two functions reference it (`_post_invoice_customer_scope_impl`, `_post_invoice_group_customer_scope_impl`) and every user-facing entry point funnels through those — `post_invoice` → `_post_invoice_idem_impl_20260721` → `_post_invoice_public_impl_20260718` → customer-scope → impl; `post_invoice_group` → group-customer-scope → impl; `batch_post_invoices` loops `post_invoice`. One `CREATE OR REPLACE` therefore closes all three, and the precondition asserts that caller set by name so a fourth path cannot appear unnoticed. The single writer that sets `'posted'` itself, `_post_deleted_delivery_recovery_invoice_20260719`, already requires `d.status = 'completed'`; the precondition asserts that too, and separately asserts that **no other** function writes `invoices.status = 'posted'`. **The gate.** When `invoices.delivery_id IS NOT NULL`, the delivery row is read and the post is refused with `DELIVERY_MISSING` if the row is gone, or `DELIVERY_NOT_COMPLETED` if it is soft-deleted or its status is anything other than `'completed'`. The refusal text names the delivery and tells the office what to do — complete the delivery, which is the same step that corrects the invoice to the quantities actually delivered, or void the draft if the delivery was cancelled or voided. **The delivery row is read without a lock, deliberately.** `_complete_delivery_authorized_impl` locks the delivery *before* the invoice, and the posting impl already holds `FOR UPDATE` on the invoice; taking a delivery lock inside the gate would reverse that order and deadlock. The residual window is stated honestly rather than talked around, and it is **not** one-directional: if a delivery is voided or soft-deleted in the instant between the unlocked read and the invoice UPDATE, the post proceeds even though the gate would have refused it a moment later. That is the same outcome as today's un-gated behaviour and is not made worse by this file, and `void_delivery` already has its own handling for a delivery whose invoice is already posted. Closing the window properly means reordering the locks inside `_complete_delivery_authorized_impl`, which is a separate change with its own deadlock analysis. **What is deliberately not gated, and why.** Job, application and standalone invoices carry no `delivery_id` and are untouched. Order-level invoices created by `_create_invoice_from_order_impl_20260718` also carry no `delivery_id`, and that function already **refuses to run** when the order has any delivery not in ('cancelled','voided') — it is the deliberate "cancel the deliveries and bill the whole order by hand" escape, and live currently holds 0 invoices of that shape. Gating those on `orders.status = 'fulfilled'` was considered and rejected from live evidence: every writer of that column is delivery-driven, so an order with no deliveries can never reach `'fulfilled'` and such invoices would become permanently unpostable. Credit memos and finance charges are unaffected. **Fails closed on drift.** Because `CREATE OR REPLACE` rewrites the whole body, the precondition pins the live body to md5 `9479af0a5477e89266e0264da44c766c` — the exact text read from production on 2026-08-11 — and **every** other arm aborts: an unrecognised hash aborts telling the applier to re-derive from current live source, and — deliberately — so does the already-installed case, because a second apply would overwrite whatever the live body has become since with the copy frozen on 2026-08-11. A sibling session's fix cannot be silently reverted by this file. **Atomicity is explicit.** The whole file runs inside one top-level `BEGIN;`/`COMMIT;`, the shape 30 already-applied migrations in this repo use. Without it, a non-transactional applier could leave the gate switched on in production while the probe below reported failure; with it, any raise anywhere reverts the function to exactly what it was. **What changes for the office: nothing breaks.** All delivery-linked invoices on live today already point at completed deliveries, so no existing draft becomes unpostable; the precondition also raises a notice counting any draft the gate would hold, so the applier sees the real number at apply time rather than trusting this sentence. **The behavioural probe runs at apply time and rolls itself back.** Its authoritative rollback evidence is **row-level**: the one invoice row and the one delivery row the probe writes are hashed whole with `md5(to_jsonb(row)::text)` before and after, and a mismatch aborts. A whole-table fingerprint of `invoices` and `deliveries` also runs but was **downgraded to a `RAISE NOTICE` after review** — under READ COMMITTED every statement takes a fresh snapshot, so an ordinary office edit committed by another session mid-apply would move a whole-table fingerprint through no fault of the probe, and aborting a money migration with "the rollback did not hold" when it did hold is the worst message this file could produce. The row hashes cover every column rather than a hand-picked list, so a future status-change trigger stamping `cancelled_at`, `last_edited_by` or similar cannot slip past; they are hashed rather than compared as text so an abort message can never print a live invoice row into a public PR. Everything else a real post writes (`financial_audit_log`, `activity_feed`, `rup_sales_records`, `notifications`, `invoice_line_share_snapshots`) is covered by the subtransaction rollback itself, which unwinds every table equally; the only genuinely irreversible cost is any sequence value consumed, which is cosmetic. Under a real active admin's forged `request.jwt.claims`, and bracketing each delivery-status UPDATE with a transaction-local `app.admin_override` that is **cleared again before every post attempt** so the gate is always exercised without it — the live transition trigger allows only scheduled→in_progress/cancelled and in_progress→completed/cancelled, so without that override the probe could not move the row at all and an earlier draft of this file could never have applied — it (a) flips a postable delivery-linked draft's delivery to `scheduled` — the exact quick-delivery shape this file exists to stop — and then to `cancelled`, requiring `DELIVERY_NOT_COMPLETED` each time, (b) soft-deletes the delivery while leaving its status `'completed'` and requires refusal — proving the deleted-check is not shadowed by the status check — and (c) restores the delivery and **posts for real**, requiring the invoice to reach `'posted'`, so the allow path is proved and not merely the deny path. That allow arm surfaces `SQLERRM` in its failure message so an unrelated cause, such as a closed accounting period, is not misread as the gate. **The ACL is made active, not merely asserted.** `REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public._post_invoice_impl_20260714(uuid, text) FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role` runs after the `CREATE OR REPLACE` (which preserves existing grants). The live ACL read `postgres=X/postgres` on 2026-08-11, so this is a no-op today; it is here because this is the *inner* implementation — the customer-scope and idempotency-payload-binding checks live in its two SECURITY DEFINER callers — so any client role holding EXECUTE here would skip those as well as reaching this gate. The postcondition then asserts no client role holds EXECUTE, closing the B7/B8/B9 shape by construction rather than by inspection. **Catalog lookups avoid `pg_get_function_identity_arguments()`.** The LIVE-DATA GUARD denies that builtin as "not a known read-only function", which would make this block impossible to rehearse read-only before applying (the guard runs on the ad-hoc SQL path only, so it would not have blocked the apply itself — but losing the dry-run on a money file is not a trade worth making); the signature assertion is written as plain catalog column reads (`p.pronargs = 2 AND p.proargtypes[0] = 'uuid'::regtype AND p.proargtypes[1] = 'text'::regtype`), which is exactly equivalent and calls nothing. **Second review round (2026-08-11), both reviewers, 0 BLOCKERs.** Both independently found the same real defect and it is fixed: the completeness assertions counted the definitions that *matched* a guard and required the count to be 1, which passes in exactly the case they exist to catch — add a second, unguarded overload beside a guarded one and the count is unchanged. PRECOND F, G and P are now inverted to **count the definitions that FAIL the guard and require zero**, each paired with a new existence assertion (F2, G2, P2) so that deleting the function outright cannot satisfy a zero-failures test vacuously. Recovered and landed 2026-08-12. **Adds a column:** `quote_items.cost_at_quote_cents bigint NULL`. Fixes quote-time cost drift — `save_quote`'s recompute CTE priced cost from *live* `products.current_cost` on every save and the QuoteBuilder recalc overwrote a loaded line's cost with the live catalog cost, so editing an old quote to fix a note or change acres silently repriced its cost, profit and net margin at today's cost, and `convert_quote_to_order` then copied that drifted cost into `order_items.cost_per_unit`. The quote no longer answered "what did we expect to pay when we quoted this?". Mirrors `order_items.cost_at_time_cents` (`20260513050000`): a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger stamps `ROUND(products.current_cost * 100)` **unconditionally**, ignoring any client-supplied value — a direct PostgREST insert is rep-reachable through the `qitems_insert` RLS policy, so a supplied value is untrusted. The only exception is the transaction-local setting `crx.quote_cost_snapshot_passthrough = '1'`, settable only inside a server-side transaction (`save_quote` sets it; PostgREST clients cannot), and only when a non-NULL value was supplied. Existing rows are backfilled from `ROUND(current_cost * 100)`. Known edge, recorded rather than papered over: a legacy row whose `current_cost` is NULL stays NULL, and the immutability guard then blocks any later in-place repair — fixing one requires a new migration that drops and recreates the guard. Because `save_quote` delete-and-reinserts `quote_items`, which would wipe the snapshot on every save, it captures the prior lines first, keyed strictly by quote-item id carrying each row's `product_id`; a reinserted row keeps a preserved snapshot only when the payload echoes a prior item id **and** that prior item's `product_id` matches, so an id echo cannot attach one line's snapshot to a different product. When the id does not resolve, the row falls back to the first unconsumed prior line of the same quote and same product, each prior row consumed at most once, and every valid echoed id is reserved in a full pass over the payload **before** any fallback runs — without that pass, payload order decided which line kept its snapshot, and changing a line from product A to B on a quote already holding B let the changed row claim the existing B row's cost. **Registry follow-up (historical — closed 2026-08-13):** at recovery time this column was absent from `.claude/schema-registry.json` and `src/types/index.ts`; both now declare it — see the closure note in `docs/manual/KNOWN_ISSUES.md`. |
