Test `T28` replays the corrected row and asserts those two totals, so all four live rows are now proved by execution to save with the correct money. The check is **not** deleted: zero rows is a property of the data on one day, not of the migration, so whoever applies it re-runs the count in the file header and requires zero. Risk in proportion: that row belongs to a test product on an already-`invoiced` job, not to live customer work. Behaviour test `T1` — which formerly asserted that this live shape SAVES — now asserts the refusal, so the obligation is pinned by an executable test rather than by prose; T18 and T19 pin the two exemptions. **Round-7 review changes.** Three independent reviewers converged on the same two defects in the round-6 commits, and both were real. (α) The zero-quantity skip sat **below** the new blank-unit refusal, so a line with a blank `unit`, a filled-in price and `quantity = 0` was refused — a false refusal that blocks the WHOLE job save, and reachable from the ordinary UI, since `reconcileChemAutofillUnits` leaves `unit` blank on its fallback path while the tier price is already populated. The skip moved above the refusal and `T20` pins it; a mutant that moves it back turns `T20` red by name. (β) The pre-apply obligation query in the file header tested only a blank `unit`, so it under-counted the shapes the refusal actually catches; it now reproduces `normalize_rate_unit`'s stripping on the rate side as well. Three successive versions of that query were wrong before the fourth was right; all four returned the same **one** live row, so Mason's data obligation never changed. (γ) Found by the mutation phase rather than by a reviewer: the postflight tested `anon` **before** `PUBLIC`, and a grant to `PUBLIC` reaches every role — so a PUBLIC grant reported itself as `POSTFLIGHT_ANON_EXECUTE`, naming one role while every role was exposed and pointing a reader at the wrong `REVOKE`. The broadest grant is now reported first. (δ) The from-scratch-rebuild narrative that justified the ACL statements is **withdrawn as unproven** — this file has never been replayed from scratch, and an unrun scenario is not evidence. The *reason* first given for withdrawing it was itself wrong and is corrected in round 8, not quietly dropped. **Round-8 review changes — a real BLOCKER from the exact-SHA `gpt-5.6-sol` proof gate.** The non-acre denominator rule accepted **stacked** denominators. It asked whether the rate unit *ends in* a per-acre suffix and stopped there, so `oz/cwt/ac` satisfied every exclusion — and the base derivation then took everything before the **first** slash and silently discarded `cwt`. The line normalised to a plain `oz`, compared EQUAL to a stock `unit` of `oz`, and **saved**: a per-hundredweight rate billed as though it were per-acre, which is the exact money error the rule exists to stop. `oz per cwt per acre` was the same hole one spelling away. Reproduced in the container *before* the fix (`T24` reported `refused=f`), so this was demonstrated rather than argued. The test is now written **subtractively**: remove one trailing per-acre suffix in either spelling, then refuse if any `/` or whole-word `per` *survives*. That is strictly stronger and refuses nothing the exclusion form accepted — every legitimate spelling reduces to a bare unit, and a hyphenated unit such as `fl-oz` carries no separator to survive. `T24` and `T25` pin both stacked forms and a mutant reverting to the exclusion form turns `T24` red. **A latent defect in the prover itself was found in the same pass:** mutants were applied with `String.replace(from, to)`, and JavaScript treats `$&`, `` $` ``, `$'` and `$1` in a *string* replacement as substitution patterns — SQL regex literals here end in `$'` routinely, so such a mutant silently spliced the whole remainder of the migration into itself and failed to install with a syntax error far from the edit. The replacement now goes through a function and is taken verbatim. **Two further round-8 gate findings, one rejected on evidence and one accepted and still open.** *Rejected:* the gate reported that a clean replay cannot apply this migration because migration 20260215200000 leaves an undropped five-argument `save_job` overload. That premise is false — migration `20260331600000` (the RPC-overload consolidation) collects every `save_job` overload, **drops all of them**, and recreates a single unified one, and each later migration (20260530020452, 20260609190820, 20260624120000, 20260706020000, 20260706080000) recreates the same six-argument signature; live carries exactly one overload, `pronargs` 6, read read-only 2026-08-24. Recorded as a disagreement rather than silently resolved. The same false premise had been repeated in this row at (δ) above, sourced from an earlier round, and is corrected there. *Accepted and now CLOSED, on Mason's 2026-08-24 scope decision:* `save_job` did its idempotency lookup with an unlocked `SELECT` scoped to `operation = 'save_job'` and recorded with `ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) DO NOTHING`, while the live unique constraint is on `idempotency_key` **alone** (`idempotency_keys_idempotency_key_key`, verified live 2026-08-24). So a key already spent by another operation was invisible to the lookup, the mutation proceeded, the receipt insert was swallowed by the conflict, and the next retry mutated **again** — a duplicate job, and therefore a duplicate bill; two callers racing on one key could also both pass the unlocked lookup. That block was **byte-identical to the pre-change live body** (`20260706080000` lines 87–97 and 297–301), so it is a pre-existing defect this migration now closes rather than one it caused. The lookup routes through `check_idempotency(text, text)` — SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path` pinned, md5 `2c93efc82ad63c906eab944e8b70c88e`, already used by `draw_down_quote` and others — which takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the key and raises `IDEMPOTENCY_CROSS_OP_KEY_REUSE`. **No grant change:** the helper is executable by `postgres` and `service_role` only, and `save_job` is SECURITY DEFINER owned by `postgres`, so the inner call runs with the owner's rights. Mason chose to fold this in rather than issue a follow-up migration, because a second migration replacing this same function body is the non-atomic hazard round 3 already caught. The tail `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) DO NOTHING` is deliberately kept: a conflict can now only be a same-operation row, because cross-operation reuse raises before any mutation. `T26` pins the cross-operation refusal **and** that it wrote nothing, and `T27` pins that an ordinary same-key replay still returns the same job and creates no second one. The preflight additionally asserts the helper is installed (`PREFLIGHT_MISSING_HELPER`) — PL/pgSQL resolves the call at run time, so without that assertion a database lacking the helper would accept this migration cleanly and fail on the first real job save; a mutation phase drops the helper and requires the apply to abort. **Round-9 change — Mason's scope decision on 2026-08-24, taken on top of round 8 rather than deferred.** Round 8 stopped the *cross-operation* duplicate, but `check_idempotency(text, text)` matches on key and operation **only**, so a key already spent by an earlier `save_job` and then reused for a **different job or an edited payload** still returned the earlier success. That is the quieter half of the same defect: nothing is duplicated, but the current request is never saved and the operator is told it was — an edited quantity, cent amount or job header silently discarded. It also let any caller replay another user's receipt. The call now goes to `check_idempotency_intent(text, text, uuid, text)`, which additionally binds the **calling actor** and a **sha256 request fingerprint**. This is not a new helper written for this migration: it is installed live and already called by nine money RPCs — `create_return`, `approve_return`, `receive_return`, `reject_return`, `cancel_return`, `issue_return_credit`, `create_commission_payment`, `post_commission_payment`, `void_commission_payment` (read read-only from `pg_proc` 2026-08-24, md5 of the live body `edc73be809069669e8441eba7acf443d`). Four mechanics that a reader must not have to rediscover. (i) It returns a **wrapper** `{"found": true, "result": ...}`, not the bare result that `check_idempotency` returns, so the body unwraps `-> 'result'` and raises `IDEMPOTENCY_RESULT_INVALID` on a receipt that is not an object carrying a `job_id`, rather than handing a malformed cached value back to the app. (ii) The fingerprint is hashed with `extensions.digest`, **schema-qualified on purpose**: pgcrypto lives in the `extensions` schema on this project while this body pins `search_path` to `public, pg_temp`, so an unqualified call would resolve at run time and fail on the first keyed save. The preflight asserts `extensions.digest(bytea, text)` exists for exactly that reason. (iii) Both `p_fields` and `p_chemicals` are order-normalised (`jsonb_agg(e ORDER BY e::text)`) before hashing, so a client that re-serialises the same grid in a different order still replays instead of being refused. (iv) The helper **fails closed** on a receipt carrying neither binding column — a pre-binding receipt's intent cannot be reconstructed, so it raises `IDEMPOTENCY_INTENT_MISMATCH` rather than replaying blind; the receipt write therefore stamps `request_fingerprint` and `request_actor_id` and raises `IDEMPOTENCY_RECEIPT_MISSING` if that stamp lands on no row. The preflight gained three assertions accordingly: the helper, `extensions.digest`, and both receipt columns on `idempotency_keys` — all `PREFLIGHT_MISSING_HELPER`, all refusing the apply rather than deferring the failure to the first real job save. `T29` pins the actor mismatch (a seeded receipt bound to a different actor is refused and writes nothing), `T30` pins A→B→A payload reuse (the changed payload is refused, and the original payload still replays to the same job), and two mutants pin the code: reverting to the key-only helper turns `T30` red, and dropping the receipt binding turns `T27` red. **A defect in the test suite was found by that second mutant and is worth recording**, because it is the same class as the round-7 postflight ordering bug — the prover found it, no reviewer did. With the receipt left unbound, the helper's fail-closed clause turns an *ordinary retry* into a hard `IDEMPOTENCY_INTENT_MISMATCH`; `T27` had no handler, so the whole test file aborted on a raw psql error and the mutation phase could not attribute the failure to any test. A mutant that fails the suite without failing its own test proves nothing. `T27` now carries the same `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` handler `T20` does, converting an outright refusal into a named `T27 FAIL`. **Round-10 change — a BLOCKER from the exact-SHA `gpt-5.6-sol` proof gate, and the second time this gate has caught a real money hole.** The unit guard could treat FLUID ounces as DRY ounces. `normalize_rate_unit` collapses `fl oz` to `oz` knowing nothing about the product, and the equality shortcut `CONTINUE WHEN v_qty_unit = v_price_unit` sat **above** the `product_form` lookup — so a DRY product carrying `rate_unit = 'fl oz/ac'` against a stock `unit` of `'oz'` normalised to `oz = oz`, took the shortcut, and billed with nothing proven. The alias is correct on a LIQUID product — the live `unit_conversions` table records `oz` as "alias for fl oz", both liquid, both factor 1 — but on a dry product `oz` is a dry ounce (a WEIGHT) while `fl oz` is a VOLUME, and `field_app_priced_quantity`, the authoritative converter the rest of the app bills through, **refuses the pair**: its dry branch sizes `fl oz` as NULL, i.e. not convertible. The guard was therefore **more lenient than the SQL that does the billing**, passing a shape its own converter calls unpriceable. An earlier round of this work had examined the same alias and cleared it — correctly, but only for liquids; the dry case was never checked, and that gap is why this is recorded rather than quietly fixed. **The fix is an ordering change plus a narrow refusal.** The `product_form` lookup moves ABOVE the shortcut, and a new `CHEM_UNIT_FORM_MISMATCH` fires only where the shortcut would otherwise have fired, only on a dry product, only when a fluid-ounce spelling is what made the two sides equal, and only when the two RAW spellings actually differ — because `field_app_priced_quantity` returns the quantity untouched whenever the raw units are identical, so `fl oz` against `fl oz` is self-consistent and must keep saving. **The wider form was considered and rejected**, and the reason is worth keeping: gating the shortcut on "the converter must agree" would refuse a LIQUID product priced in pounds (`lbs/ac` against `lb` normalise equal while the liquid size table carries no pound at all), and a false refusal blocks the WHOLE job save — the round-7 defect three reviewers caught. `T31` and `T32` pin both directions of the dry refusal, `T33` pins that the liquid pair still saves and `T34` that an identically-spelled dry pair still saves — the two false-refusal guards — and a mutant removing the rule turns `T31` red by name. **Reachability, stated at its real size:** no live product carries `fl oz` on a dry form today (read read-only 2026-08-24 — the 85 dry products use `dry oz`, `lb`, `mg` and `oz`), but the units compared here come from `p_chemicals`, not from the product catalog, and EXECUTE is granted to `authenticated`, so a hand-built call reaches the shape however clean the catalog is. Two OTHER findings the same gate raised in the round before were **rejected on evidence**: it reported that this branch rolls back the `codex-review` skill hardening and deletes the draw-tier real-schema prover. Both are artifacts of a stale base — the proof wrapper compares a snapshot of `origin/main` against a snapshot of HEAD, which is a two-dot comparison, so everything `main` gained since the merge base (PRs #448 and #442 here) reads as a rollback by the branch. The three-dot diff `origin/main...HEAD` is EMPTY for every file named, and merging current `origin/main` made both findings disappear. **Round-11 change — a second BLOCKER from the exact-SHA `gpt-5.6-sol` gate, and it reopened the round-8 rule.** The stacked-denominator refusal accepted a **MIXED-format** stack. The probe ran both strip patterns unconditionally, so `oz per acre/ac` — oz per acre PER ACRE, one denominator in each spelling — lost `/ac` to the slash pattern and then ` per acre` to the word pattern, came back a bare `oz` with nothing left to catch, normalised to `oz`, matched a stock `unit` of `oz` and **billed a per-acre-squared rate as if it were per-acre**. `oz-per-acre/ac` was the same hole one separator away. Round 8 had stated the rule correctly — "strip exactly one trailing per-acre suffix, then refuse if any denominator survives" — and then implemented two unconditional strips, which is a different rule: *remove one and refuse what survives* is not *remove every spelling and then look*. The second strip is now conditional on the first not having fired, which makes it singular as designed. `T35` and `T36` pin both mixed spellings and a mutant restoring the two unconditional passes turns `T35` red by name. The base derivation below the probe is deliberately left as split_part-plus-one-strip and is no longer claimed to be character-identical to the probe: anything reaching it has already been proved to carry at most one per-acre denominator in one spelling, because a stacked or mixed form is refused above and never arrives. **A MEDIUM from the same verdict was also fixed rather than argued down:** the container prover used a FIXED container name and ran `docker rm -f` on it before establishing that the container was its own, so a developer's unrelated container of that name would have been destroyed. The name is now unique per run, every container the prover starts carries a `crx.prover` label, and the only broad sweep filters on that label — nothing outside this prover can match. **Round-12 change — the round-10 fix was a HALF-fix, and the gate returned the other half as a fresh HIGH.** Round 10 made the fluid-ounce alias form-aware, but only at the **equality shortcut**. The shortcut is not the only way a fluid ounce reaches the money, and the path it missed is the worse one. `field_app_priced_quantity` is called with `v_qty_unit` and `v_price_unit` — the **NORMALISED** units — so `fl oz` has already become `oz` before the converter ever sees it. Handed the raw `fl oz` the converter's dry branch sizes it NULL and refuses; handed `oz` it sizes it 1 and converts **16:1 into pounds**. So a DRY product with `rate_unit = 'fl oz/ac'` against a stock `unit` of `'lb'` never took the shortcut at all — the two do not normalise equal — it went down the conversion path, turned a VOLUME into a WEIGHT, and derived the authoritative cost and price totals from it. Normalisation had erased the exact distinction the converter would have refused on. The rule is now **unconditional**: on a dry product, a fluid-ounce spelling on either side is refused, whatever the other side says and whether or not the two normalise equal. That is simpler as well as strictly stronger, and it has no precondition left to get wrong — a dry product has no density in this system, so nothing downstream can carry a fluid ounce into a weight honestly. **A test had to be INVERTED, and that is the part worth remembering.** Round 10's `T34` *required* a dry line quoted `fl oz` on both sides to SAVE, reasoning that identical spellings are self-consistent and the converter passes them untouched. Writing that exemption into a test **froze the half-fix in place** and would have defended it against the next reviewer. Self-consistent arithmetic on a unit the inventory and invoice sides cannot convert is not a saving grace. `T34` now asserts the refusal; `T37` (against `lb`) and `T38` (against `dry oz`) pin the conversion path the half-fix left open; `T33` still pins that the LIQUID `fl oz`/`oz` pair saves, which is the line that must not move; and the mutant that removes the rule now turns `T37` red — the conversion path — rather than `T31`. Proof re-run green at 44 behaviour tests and 24 mutation phases (18 turn a named test red, 6 abort the apply). **Round 15 (2026-08-24) closed three HIGH money findings from the exact-SHA gpt-5.6-sol gate, the first of which is the largest hole this file has carried.** (1) EQUAL UNITS WERE A FREE PASS FOR THE QUANTITY: the shortcut `CONTINUE WHEN v_qty_unit = v_price_unit` proved only that both sides were counted in the same unit and nothing about how many, so a hand-built call sending 10 acres at 2 oz/ac with quantity 200 oz passed and the derived totals authoritatively stored 20,000 cents instead of 2,000 — the caller setting the money directly, on the one path this migration exists to close. The mismatched branch already refused a quantity that is not `rate x acres`; applying that rule only when the units DIFFER was the inconsistency, since the quantity reaches the totals either way. The equal-units branch now derives `rate x acres` DIRECTLY, without calling the converter — the units are identical so no conversion is owed, and routing through it would make an exotic-but-self-consistent unit the tables do not carry (`cc` against `cc`) size NULL and start refusing a well-formed line. Verified read-only before the change that all three live BILLING rows satisfy `quantity = rate x acres` exactly (JOB-2026-0002 73.31 = 1 x 73.31; -0003 and -0004 3752.64 = 96 x 39.09), so this refuses nothing that exists; JOB-2026-0001 carries quantity 0 and is exempt earlier. `T42` pins it. A RESIDUAL is recorded rather than hidden: with no usable rate or acreage there is nothing to derive from, so such a line still saves on the caller's quantity — closing that means ruling a priced line with no acreage invalid, which is an operational policy call for Mason and is NOT taken here. (2) LEADING `per` FORMS evaded both the denominator and unspecified-unit guards, because every pattern required whitespace or a hyphen BEFORE `per`; `per cwt` therefore survived normalisation and, against a stock unit carrying the same text, reached the equality shortcut and billed. An earlier round had added this shape to the PRE-APPLY query but not to the runtime guard — fixing the report and leaving the enforcement, which is the half-fix pattern this file has now repeated three times. `T43` pins it. (3) HYPHENATED FLUID OUNCES bypassed the dry guard: the fold covered whitespace, periods and zero-width characters but not hyphens, so `fl-oz` on both sides of a dry line normalised equal and billed a volume as a weight. Third spelling escape in three rounds, which is why the fold now covers the separator class rather than the characters named so far. `T44` pins it. The prover also gained a structural fix worth more than any single mutant: an AMBIGUOUS mutation anchor is now refused outright unless the mutant opts into `all`. `String.replace` takes the first match, so when the acreage finiteness bound came to exist in two places the mutant silently edited the copy it did not mean, left the real guard standing, and the phase reported `MUTATION NOT DETECTED` — which reads as "this test is weak" and invites deleting a test that was simply never reached. **Round 16 (2026-08-24) ENDED THE CLASS rather than closing one more instance of it, and the table is the argument.** Four consecutive rounds of the dry fluid-ounce rule were defeated by a separator that had not been enumerated yet: round 12 (three literal spellings) by `fl. oz`; round 13 (+ periods and whitespace) by `fl<U+200B>oz`; round 14 (+ zero-width and NBSP) by `fl-oz`; round 15 (+ ASCII hyphen) by `fl<U+2010>oz`. U+2010 and U+2011 are hyphens and U+202F is a narrow no-break space, and Unicode carries many more — so enumeration could never converge, because the caller picks the character and the list is always written afterwards. The fold now has NO character list at all: everything that is not a letter or a digit is DELETED and what remains must BE the word (`floz`, `fluidounce`). No separator survives, so none can be smuggled in. Verified on live PostgreSQL 17.6 across 31 spellings — all 19 fluid-ounce forms refuse (every escape from rounds 12-15, plus `fl_oz` and `f l o z`), and all 12 legitimate units pass untouched including `dry oz`, `dry<NBSP>oz`, `dry-oz`, `gal.` and the near-miss `flour oz`, which folds to `flouroz` and fails the anchored `fl|fluid` prefix. `T45` pins U+2010, U+2011 and U+202F; a mutant narrowing the fold back to an ASCII list reddens `T45` ALONE while `T39`/`T40`/`T44` stay green, which is what makes the list-free property itself the thing under test rather than any one character. The NBSP reasoning recorded in round 14 is now moot and is marked as such in the migration: separation no longer matters, since `dry oz` folds to `dryoz` and simply fails the prefix. **Round 17 (2026-08-24) closed the UNDER-billing mirror of the round-15 finding, and settled the policy question both of them raised.** The gate found (HIGH) that the zero-quantity exit was UNCONDITIONAL: a line with a real price, a real rate and real acreage but `quantity = 0` saved and billed NOTHING, and the zero propagated into the invoice. Where the equality shortcut let a caller OVER-charge, this let one UNDER-charge. It also found that `T20` encoded the defect as correct behaviour — it submitted 10 acres at 1.5 pt/ac with a price and quantity 0 and required the total to stay 0 — the second test in this file written around a defect that would have defended it against the next reviewer. **Mason's decision (2026-08-24): refuse only where a customer's money is actually at stake.** Implemented as two refusals and three exemptions. `CHEM_QUANTITY_ZERO_BUT_EXPECTED` refuses a zero-quantity line that carries a PRICE when a positive quantity was derivable; `CHEM_QUANTITY_UNVERIFIABLE` refuses a priced line whose quantity cannot be checked at all — which closes the bypass round 15 left open, where a hand-built call could switch the entire check off by simply OMITTING the rate and naming any quantity (a guard the caller can disable is not a guard). The three exemptions are each a case where zero is genuinely right: `customer_supplied` (contributes 0 by definition); NO PRICE (nothing can be under-charged — this is what keeps live JOB-2026-0001 saveable, since it carries quantity 0 against a 5/ac rate over 178.31 acres with a cost but price 0, and `T47` pins that shape); and NO usable rate or acreage (nothing was EXPECTED, which is the ordinary-UI path `T20` exists for, where a product is picked before any acreage is entered — there acres = 0 so the expected quantity is 0 too). `T20` was corrected to carry acres 0, the scenario it was always about. A recorded, accepted residual: a cost-only line (price 0) can still misstate MARGIN through an unverified quantity, which follows directly from conditioning on price. `T46` pins the underbilling refusal, `T48` the unverifiable-quantity refusal, `T47` the live-shape exemption, and each has a mutant reddening its own test. The prover also gained an AMBIGUOUS-ANCHOR check in round 15 that earned its keep immediately here: three mutants had anchors that this round made non-unique or stale, and each was refused loudly instead of silently editing the wrong site and reporting a good test as weak. **Round 18 (2026-08-24) closed two more HIGH money findings, one of which is the subtlest in the file.** (1) THE QUANTITY TOLERANCE WAS A PERCENTAGE. Both comparison sites allowed `GREATEST(0.0001, |expected| * 1e-6)` -- one part per million. Nothing bounds rate, acreage or quantity, so that is not a small amount: it is a fraction of whatever the caller chose. At 5,000 acres and a rate of 1e9 the accepted difference is 5,000,000 units, i.e. a $5,000,000 discrepancy at $1 a unit, waved through by a rule whose stated purpose was rounding. It is now a FIXED `0.0001` at both sites -- exactly the precision `fmt4` stores the quantity at, and nothing more. `T50` pins that a 5-unit shortfall on a 10,000,000-unit line is refused; `T51` pins that a genuine 0.0001 rounding difference on that same line still SAVES, because over-tightening a money guard is its own defect. A side effect worth recording: the fixed tolerance independently closes the NaN-acreage bypass, since `abs(v_qty - NaN) <= 0.0001` is FALSE where `NaN <= GREATEST(0.0001, NaN)` was TRUE. The finiteness-bound mutant had to restore BOTH halves to reproduce the original defect, and the prover refused to score a detection it had not earned until it did -- two independent guards now stand between a NaN acreage and the money. (2) DENOMINATOR SEPARATORS WERE ENUMERATED, the same defect that beat the fluid-ounce rule four times, in the per-acre rule: it looked for `per` bounded by whitespace or hyphens, so `oz_per_cwt` and `oz.per.cwt` matched nothing, survived normalisation, and with a stock `unit` carrying the same text reached the equality branch and billed a PER-HUNDREDWEIGHT rate as per-acre. Separators are now folded to single spaces before the denominator is classified (`/` deliberately preserved, since there it is the denominator marker rather than a separator), after which the existing subtractive logic runs against exactly one spelling. The canonical form also subsumes the leading-`per` arm added in round 15, so that arm was REMOVED rather than left as a second way of saying the same thing. `T49` pins both spellings; every existing denominator test (`T13`, `T24`, `T25`, `T35`, `T36`, `T43`) and both legitimate per-acre tests (`T5`, `T10`) stay green. That is the third distinct place in this file where enumerating characters failed, which is a pattern rather than three coincidences. **Round 19 (2026-08-24) closed an ASYMMETRY in the fluid-ounce rule (HIGH) and bound the audit identity to the idempotency key (MEDIUM).** (1) The rate side reached the fluid-ounce check already stripped of its denominator (`v_rate_base`), but the stock side was passed in RAW. A dry line with `rate_unit = 'oz/ac'` against a stock `unit` of `'fl oz/ac'` therefore folded to `flozac`, missed the anchored pattern, and `normalize_rate_unit` then collapsed BOTH sides to `oz` anyway -- so the equality branch accepted it and derived authoritative money from a VOLUME price on a weight product. The defect was the asymmetry itself: two sides of one comparison were being canonicalised differently. `'fl oz/ac'` still DENOTES fluid ounces -- a denominator says per what, not what -- so each side is now reduced to the unit it names before the concept test runs: fold separators (keeping `/` meaningful), drop everything from the first `/`, drop a spelled-out `per ...` tail, then delete every remaining non-alphanumeric. Verified on live PostgreSQL 17.6 across 21 spellings: all 8 fluid-ounce forms refuse including `fl oz/ac`, `fl oz per acre`, `fluid ounce per acre`, `fl-oz/ac` and `fl.oz/ac`, while every legitimate unit passes untouched including the denominator forms `oz/ac`, `lb/ac`, `gal/ac`, `pt/ac`, `dry oz/ac` and the near-miss `flour oz/ac`. `T52` pins it. (2) `p_performed_by` was missing from the idempotency fingerprint although it is written to `jobs.created_by`. The actor check only refuses that parameter when it is non-NULL AND disagrees with the caller, so flipping it between NULL and the authenticated actor passes the check while changing what the row records -- and on a spent key the earlier receipt replayed, silently discarding the new audit identity. That is the identical silent-discard shape round 9 fixed for the payload, missed here because this parameter is not part of the payload. It is now fingerprinted separately from `v_actor`, because it is a different input with a different effect. `T53` pins it. **Two mutants had to be strengthened rather than the code weakened**, both for the same reason and both caught by the prover refusing to score an unearned detection: the round-19 denominator-stripping wrapper carries its OWN list-free fold, so narrowing only the outer fold left Unicode separators still handled (`T45` stayed green until the mutant narrowed both), and the round-18 fixed tolerance independently closes the NaN-acreage path (`T11` stayed green until the mutant restored the relative tolerance too). In both cases two independent guards now stand between the defect and the money. **Round 20 (2026-08-24) ended the separator-bypass class for the DENOMINATOR rule, and disproved a BLOCKER.** The gate raised two items. The first was a BLOCKER claiming this branch had replaced the proof generator's fixed Git executable and isolated environment with ambient `execFileSync("git", ...)`, dropped `--no-replace-objects`, and deleted the hostile-global-config regression test -- i.e. that a future packet could be steered by untrusted Git state to mint a clean proof for unreviewed code. **It is a FALSE POSITIVE from a stale base, and the three-dot diff disproves it in one command:** `git diff --stat origin/main...HEAD -- scripts/write-codex-push-proof.mjs` is EMPTY, so this branch never touched the file. `origin/main` had gained 3 commits since the merge base, one of which HARDENED that very file, and the review packet compares snapshots -- structurally a two-dot diff -- so main's own improvement renders as "the candidate deleted this". Resolved by MERGING `origin/main`, not by editing anything; hand-restoring a security-critical file to match a version never read is how the warned-about vulnerability would actually get introduced. This is the documented trap recorded in the branch handoff, and it has now produced three confident false BLOCKERS across this work. The second finding was REAL (HIGH): the denominator canon preserves ASCII `/` and folds every other punctuation mark to a space, so `oz<U+2215>cwt` -- a Unicode DIVISION SLASH -- became `oz cwt` with no `/` and no whole-word `per` surviving, and a PER-HUNDREDWEIGHT rate would bill as per-acre. Rather than add slash homoglyphs to a list -- which would have been the FIFTH round of the game the fluid-ounce list lost four times -- the question changed. What separates `oz cwt` from a legitimate `fl oz` is not the character between the words, it is that `oz cwt` IS NOT A UNIT. A priced line must now name a unit the system recognises: either `normalize_rate_unit` maps it to a canonical unit (`oz`, `pt`, `qt`, `gal`, `lb`, `ton`, `g`, `kg`, `l`, `ml`) or the folded spelling exists in the live `unit_conversions` table. A denominator smuggled in behind ANY separator, including ones not yet invented, leaves a residue that is not a unit and is refused. Gated on PRICE per Mason's 2026-08-24 rule; the recorded residual is unchanged (a cost-only line can still misstate margin), and live JOB-2026-0001 carries `rate_unit` `'32'` with price 0 and stays saveable for exactly that reason. `T54` pins three homoglyphs (U+2215, U+2044, U+FF0F); `T55` pins that all six live unit spellings still SAVE on a priced line, and it is deliberately built so that BOTH arms of the rule are load-bearing -- `dry oz` survives only via `unit_conversions` (no `normalize_rate_unit` arm) and `ton` only via the canonical-output arm (no `unit_conversions` row). The harness gained a `unit_conversions` table mirroring the live 11 spellings, because a proof against a different allowlist would prove nothing about production. **Round 21 (2026-08-24) is the most instructive round in this file: the FOLD ITSELF was doing the attacker's work.** The gate submitted `oz<U+2215>сԝт` — a Unicode division slash followed by CYRILLIC homoglyphs for `cwt`. Under `[^a-z0-9]` every non-ASCII character is DELETED, so that string folds to a clean `oz`. The denominator was not caught, it was ERASED; `oz` is a perfectly recognised unit, both sides matched, and a per-hundredweight rate would bill as per-acre. **Every separator round from 12 to 20 rested on the assumption that folding leaves a residue behind for a rule to catch. Against non-ASCII it leaves nothing.** The fold may no longer discard anything silently: a small enumerated set is normalised first (Unicode spaces to a space, zero-width deleted) and ANY remaining character outside `[a-z0-9 ./-]` refuses a priced line with `CHEM_UNIT_UNSUPPORTED_CHARACTER`. The property that matters is the inversion — **the enumeration is for CONVENIENCE and the allowlist is for SAFETY**, so getting the convenience list wrong can only cause a REFUSAL, never an acceptance. That is the opposite of rounds 12-20, where an incomplete list meant something slipped through, and it is why this round ends the class rather than closing one more instance. Ordering is deliberate: it sits AFTER the fluid-ounce rule so a dry line written `fl<U+2010>oz` still reports the specific `CHEM_UNIT_FORM_MISMATCH` that tells the operator what is actually wrong. Verified on live PostgreSQL 17.6: the Cyrillic attack, the Unicode slash and the Unicode hyphen all refuse, while `dry<NBSP>oz` still saves and all 18 real unit spellings are untouched. `T56` pins the homoglyph (including the no-slash variant and the plain-ASCII control); `T57` was added because the round-20 backstop had lost its own oracle — the new character rule catches T54's Unicode slashes FIRST, so removing the backstop left T54 green and the prover refused the unearned detection. `T57` uses `cwt`: plain ASCII, no separator, not a fluid-ounce spelling, simply not a unit — the one case only the backstop refuses. **Round 14 (2026-08-24) closed the ZERO-WIDTH spelling of the same fluid-ounce rule, raised by CodeRabbit as a P1 hours after round 13 shipped — the same mistake twice running.** Round 13 folded periods and whitespace and stopped there, so `fl<U+200B>oz` walked through exactly as `fl. oz` had: `normalize_rate_unit` returns the unrecognised token unchanged on both sides, the equality shortcut fires, and a VOLUME is billed on a product sold by weight. The lesson recorded is the general one — round 13 fixed the single spelling a reviewer named instead of adopting the whole rule the repo already had. `src/lib/blendMathValidator.ts` defines the complete LOSSLESS set (case; zero-width U+200B/200C/200D/FEFF deleted outright; any run of real whitespace including the non-breaking space; periods) and the guard now mirrors that set exactly. Measured on live PostgreSQL 17.6, the round-13 expression missed five real forms: `fl<ZWSP>oz`, `fl<ZWNJ>oz`, `fl<ZWJ>oz`, `fl<BOM>oz`, `fluid<ZWSP> ounce`. Zero-width is DELETED (it separates nothing, so `fl<ZWSP>oz` must close to `floz`) while the non-breaking space is MAPPED TO A SPACE (it does separate, so `dry<NBSP>oz` must stay two words) — `T40` pins the refusal, `T41` pins that the legitimate `dry<NBSP>oz` line still SAVES, and a mutant removing only the zero-width fold turns `T40` red while leaving `T39` and `T41` green. Round 14 also took four further CodeRabbit findings: the postflight now checks `pg_roles` before passing a role NAME to `has_function_privilege()` (a missing role raised a raw catalog error instead of the named `POSTFLIGHT_*` assertion; a missing *required* role now raises `POSTFLIGHT_ROLE_MISSING` rather than being read as a pass, while a missing `anon` correctly satisfies its assertion); migration applies in the prover now run under `--single-transaction`, because without it a postflight mutant left the CREATE OR REPLACE committed while the prover reported the apply as aborted — the real Supabase channel rolls back and the proof had not been modelling it; stale-container reaping is restricted to terminal states so a concurrent run is no longer force-removed mid-proof; and two mutant comments that named the wrong oracle test were corrected. The pre-apply count gained a fourth term (a rate that is only a denominator, `per acre`/`per-acre`, which the earlier strip could not reduce to empty) and now carries an explicit SCOPE note: it mirrors the `CHEM_UNIT_UNSPECIFIED` refusal ONLY, so a zero there is not a claim about the other four refusals. All four live `job_chemicals` rows were therefore re-verified individually against all five rules on 2026-08-24 (read-only) and every one clears; all four products are LIQUID, so the dry fluid-ounce rule cannot fire on any of them. **Round 13 (2026-08-24) closed the PERIOD SPELLING of the same fluid-ounce rule, returned by the exact-SHA gate as a fresh P1 — the third time this one rule was caught incomplete.** Round 12 matched three literal strings (`fl oz`, `floz`, `fluid ounce`); `fl. oz` is none of them. `normalize_rate_unit` has no CASE arm for the period form, so it falls through to `ELSE base` and returns the string unchanged — both sides of a dry line quoted `fl. oz/ac` against `fl. oz` therefore normalised to the same unrecognised token, hit the equality shortcut, and derived authoritative totals from a VOLUME on a product billed by weight. Not an exotic spelling: `src/lib/blendMathValidator.ts` documents that periods are insignificant and "'fl. oz' is 'fl oz'". The rule now canonicalises both sides (runs of whitespace **and periods** collapse to one space) and matches the CONCEPT `{fl|fluid} x {oz|ozs|ounce|ounces}`, so it no longer depends on enumerating spellings; the expression is written once and applied to both sides through a `VALUES`/`unnest` list so the two copies cannot drift. Verified on live PostgreSQL 17.6 over 23 spellings: all 13 fluid-ounce forms refuse, and `oz`, `dry oz`, `lb`, `ton`, `mg`, `gal`, `pt` and the near-miss `flour oz` do not. `T39` pins the refusal, `T34` (no periods) and `T33` (the LIQUID false-refusal guard) both stay green, and a dedicated mutant that removes **only** the period folding turns `T39` red while leaving `T34` green. **Round 22 (2026-08-24) records the LIMIT OF THE WHOLE FILE, and pins a live row.** Every guard in this migration checks that units are INTERNALLY CONSISTENT; not one checks that a rate is PLAUSIBLE. Not theoretical: during the catalogue cleanup the owner was asked what rate unit `Powder Keg - Corn` should carry -- a product costing $931 per pound -- and first answered "MG per acre". A milligram is a legitimate `unit_conversions` value, so a milligram per acre satisfies EVERY refusal in this file (units consistent, characters ASCII, unit recognised, denominator per-acre, quantity = rate x acres). All twenty-one rounds of hardening pass it and the invoice comes out roughly six orders of magnitude low. The only thing that caught it was a reviewer refusing to write down an owner answer that did not look right; no amount of SQL would have. It is now a banner block in the migration header so nobody reads the file's length as coverage of that question -- closing it needs per-product plausible-rate bounds, which is separate work. A narrower instance is recorded beside it: setting `rate_unit = inventory_unit` (done for three products on 2026-08-24) makes the ARITHMETIC safe, since equal units mean no conversion occurs and no conversion factor can be wrong, but it does NOT make the rate right -- if a product's true intent is per-hundredweight, equality hides that rather than catching it. **The catalogue cleanup completed with Mason's explicit approval and is re-measured here independently:** active products with a blank `rate_unit` went 25 -> 0 (30 -> 5 in total, all five now INACTIVE and unable to reach a job), across two updates totalling 25 rows, with the money fingerprint across all 604 products identical to the cent before and after (`tier1 61499.36 | tier2 63132.85 | tier3 69185.27 | cost 53589.02`); only `rate_unit` changed and `rate_per_acre` remains NULL on every one. The catalogue now carries TEN distinct `rate_unit` values, every non-blank one a literal `unit_conversions` row. **Change 5's live exposure is therefore ZERO ACTIVE PRODUCTS, reached by fixing the data rather than by weakening the rule** -- and that is a property of the data on 2026-08-24, not of the migration: a legacy import or a hand-built call can recreate a blank tomorrow, which is exactly why the rule stays. `T58` is new and LOAD-BEARING rather than hypothetical: it pins `Accelerate Seed Treatment - Per Unit` as it now exists live -- active, billing, `product_form` NULL, stocked and rated in `Unit` -- and asserts it SAVES. It is the only test exercising three things together that nothing else touches: the NULL-form branch every form-aware rule collapses into "not dry", a unit typed `both` in `unit_conversions` so neither the liquid nor the dry table settles it, and the units-equal path where no conversion can occur. If `T58` ever goes red, that product has stopped being sellable. SQL sha256: `e858fcfe6aa7f944411e68deb324a564af555f324d53aa2e4cecd769b4868f47`. **No live migration apply is authorized by this repository change; live remains untouched.** |

> **Recovery note (2026-08-12; rows 880-885).** These six migrations were **applied to production on 2026-08-12 by concurrent sessions that never landed their files** — no branch, no worktree and no pull request carried them, so for part of a day `main` did not describe production and six migrations running against live money could not be reviewed by anyone. This is the same class of gap PR #371 closed on 2026-08-11; it reopened the next day, six files wide. The files here are **not reconstructions**. Each is the exact `apply_migration` payload recovered verbatim — five from the applying session's transcript and `20260812115238` from the live ledger — complete with its original header, preconditions and review history. **All six are published byte-identical.** `20260812115238`'s 35-row approved preimage map was withheld when the file first landed, because it lists live order-line identifiers with their prices and profit and this repository is public; Mason directed on 2026-08-14 that it be published in full, on his stated basis that the figures in this system are not real or operational. Fidelity was then proven rather than asserted: function bodies extracted from the recovered text were md5-compared against live `pg_proc.prosrc`, matching exactly and at identical length for `_guard_below_cost_approval_immutable`, `_resnapshot_order_item_cost_on_product_change`, `_snapshot_quote_item_cost` and `_guard_quote_item_cost_snapshot`. Landing them changes no production behaviour — every one is already running — it only makes the repository match what production is actually doing. The `version` column above carries the **file** timestamp, which is what the disk filename and the ordering guard key on; each row names the ledger version Supabase assigned at apply time, which is what `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` records. The six are pinned `text eol=lf` in `.gitattributes` for the same reason as the Wave A files: several carry md5 pins computed against the live catalog, and a CRLF checkout would install bytes those pins were never computed against.
>
> **Wave A remediation round 1 (2026-08-12; rows 872-877).** All six unapplied files were re-stamped above the then-verified live high-water `20260812003315`, preserving their order as `20260812010000` through `20260812060000` (that range was superseded the same day — see the re-stamp note below). This update supersedes the pre-remediation details in rows 873, 875, and 876 where they conflict: row 873 now derives `orders.total_profit` from the already-rounded price and cost (NULL components never invent zero), pins the new post-apply body at md5 `71a66b7a69ab6984efa17ec79bc9e4a5`, and its rollback probe expects `100.01 - 33.33 = 66.68` and `77.78 - 44.44 = 33.34`; row 875 extracts `_derive_job_commission_rows(numeric,jsonb)` and makes fresh job minting call it; row 876 binds correction idempotency to actor + job + canonical split + normalized reason, refuses the whole correction when any active row is paid/cancelled/payout-linked, and otherwise reconciles every pending unbatched generation through that same shared derivation while auditing row counts. The prior disposable-database proofs predate these edits and do not prove the remediated SQL; this round is intentionally static plus the local suite, and a fresh migration execution/adversarial proof remains required before any apply.

> **Wave A re-stamp round 2 (2026-08-12, after PR #383 merged as `a7ae1cc2`; rows 872-877).** All six files moved again, from `20260812010000`–`20260812060000` to **`20260813010000`–`20260813060000`**, order preserved and content otherwise unchanged. Cause: while PR #383 was in review a concurrent session applied `20260812034831_blend_ticket_order_header_runtime_assert` and `20260812034951_restore_quote_version_whole_cent_money`, moving the live high-water from `20260812003315` to **`20260812034951`** (964 ledger rows, read via MCP `list_migrations` on 2026-08-12). That left rows 872, 873 and 874 **below** the high-water, where `migration-ordering-lib.mjs` refuses them (`ts >= newestApplied` is the passing condition), and it put row 872's filename in direct prefix collision with the already-applied `20260812010000_blend_ticket_order_header_runtime_assert`. The new stamps sit a full day above the high-water deliberately — the round-1 stamps were chosen only just above it and were overtaken within hours by ordinary sibling traffic. Repo-wide references were updated in the same change (`.gitattributes` LF pins, `src/lib/waveAMoneyMigrations.test.ts` path constants, the cross-reference comments inside the six files, and the comment references in `src/lib/db.ts`, `src/lib/db.test.ts` and `src/lib/rpcContracts.test.ts`). **No SQL logic changed and no function body changed**, so every `md5(prosrc)` pin still holds: the eight intra-wave stamp references all sit in file headers or inside anonymous `$precond$`/`$postcond$` `DO` blocks, which are never stored in `pg_proc.prosrc` — verified by dollar-quote span analysis before the edit rather than assumed. Row 877's apply-time obligation to re-stamp forward **still stands**: any further sibling apply can overtake these stamps again, so `list_migrations` must be re-read immediately before an apply. Nothing was applied and no live data was touched by this change.

> **Rows 868-870 proof status (2026-08-10).** All three were executed end-to-end against a throwaway `postgres:17-alpine` container with every post-condition passing, and each post-condition was **mutation-tested**: granting `anon` EXECUTE, revoking `authenticated` EXECUTE, and tampering the pinned `_insert_commissions_for_order` helper each made the migration abort as designed. Four load-bearing claims were proven by execution rather than asserted: the constraint predicate rejects `10.005`/`NaN`/`±Infinity` and accepts `10.01` on a real table; constraining a deferred dirty column genuinely errors; the NaN ordering semantics above; and `NOT VALID` failing to shield a legacy row from a later unrelated `UPDATE`. All four migration guard hooks return `allow` on all three files and `npm run typecheck` is clean. No `src/types/index.ts` change is needed — no column or shape changes. **All three were then applied to live production on 2026-08-10, in the order 868 → 869 → 870, on Mason's explicit in-chat approval**, each through the full migration-apply proof gate (fresh same-session `rls-security-reviewer` + `migration-drift-reviewer` charters executed as real `gpt-5.6-sol` high-effort Codex runs, both CLEAN, with the proof `queryHash` matching the on-disk file). No live data changed — the three migrations only replace function bodies and add CHECK constraints. **The migrations themselves are live; what has not happened is the repository/application side — no branch merged and no front end deployed, which remains a separate approval.**
> **Wave A rows 872-874 — historical verification status (2026-08-10).** Before remediation, all three replayed clean in a disposable `public.ecr.aws/supabase/postgres:17.6.1.143` container with `--network none`; that run proved the earlier independently-rounded header body, not the derived-profit body now in row 873. The finiteness-constraint coverage in row 874 is unchanged, but the combined replay and mutation proof is stale for the edited files. **Still required before any live apply:** a fresh migration execution proof, same-session migration-apply-guard proof, real exact-SHA `gpt-5.6-sol` high-effort verdict, re-read of the live ledger high-water, and Mason's explicit in-chat approval.
> **Wave A rows 873-874 — RLS/security review (2026-08-10).** Charter review of both files returned **0 BLOCKER, 0 HIGH**: neither file is an RLS bypass, a forgeable actor, an anon-reachable `SECURITY DEFINER` mutator, or an EXECUTE-grant regression, and the privilege model of `_round_money_to_whole_cents` is unchanged (the REVOKE is byte-identical to the one live since `20260809230500`, and `CREATE OR REPLACE` preserves the ACL). Five findings were accepted and fixed rather than waived:
> * **Silent-clobber on replay** — the md5 skip branch in `20260813020000` now pins both ends; see row 873.
> * **Half-proven probe safety** — the trigger enumeration guarding the probe covered `INSERT` only while the probe also has an `UPDATE` leg, so a future unscoped `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger could have overwritten the rounded values with every assertion still passing. It now enumerates `INSERT` *or* `UPDATE` against the five siblings read from live `pg_trigger` on 2026-08-10 (`trg_stamp_commission_split_recipient_ids`, `enforce_order_status_transition`, `guard_order_customer_source_lineage`, `guard_order_delivered_activity_cancel`, `trg_order_status_change`), every one of them column-scoped to `status`, `customer_id`, `commission_split` or `deleted_at` and therefore unable to touch the money columns.
> * **Undisclosed sign laundering** — because the rounding trigger is `BEFORE`, it runs ahead of CHECK evaluation, so a `total_price` in `(-0.005, 0)` now rounds to `0.00` and passes `orders_total_price_non_negative` where it previously raised. Under one cent, judged acceptable, and now stated in the file rather than left implicit.
> * **Self-contradicting re-runnability claim** — `20260813030000` claimed "no window in which bad data could be written" while arguing 100 lines earlier that the applier must not be assumed to wrap the file in a transaction. The claim is corrected to state the window precisely (nil on first apply; open only on a retry against a database that already carries the constraints). An explicit `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` was **rejected**: if the applier has already opened a transaction, the inner `COMMIT` would commit *its* transaction early and split the migration, a worse failure than the one it would prevent.
> * **Name-only constraint lookup** — section 3 of `20260813030000`'s post-condition matched seven pre-existing whole-cent constraints on `conname` alone while sections 1 and 2 pin the table, failing the file's own stated standard. It now derives the table from the constraint-name prefix and also requires `contype = 'c'`.
>
> Two live checks the review asked for were run and passed: `public.quote_items` carries **zero** triggers, so `20260813030000`'s always-failing probe has no trigger side effect to roll back; and the `orders` trigger set is exactly the five above. One accepted non-finding: the review confirmed roughly two-thirds of the structural post-conditions read back what the same file just wrote and so cannot fail against the file as written. They are retained deliberately as a tripwire on a future edit, not as independent verification of the apply — the non-vacuous assertions are the two behavioural probes, the `has_function_privilege` check, the NaN/Infinity semantic proof, and the `pg_get_constraintdef` column-derivation read-back.
>
> **Blocked, not drafted:** deleting the `total_price` clobber in `_update_order_items_impl` (`20260617123503:274-275`, the H2 finding on row 862) is a prerequisite for ever constraining `orders.total_price`. It could not be drafted because the **live body (13278 chars) matches no tracked disk migration** — the nearest candidate is 15865 chars. That drift must be resolved before the fix can be written against a trustworthy baseline.

> **Re-issue note (2026-08-09) — rows 857-861 carry forward timestamps.** All five were authored on 2026-08-08 as `20260808150100`, `20260808150200`, `20260808150300`, `20260808150400`, and `20260808170000`, and merged to `main` before they were applied. Live ledger row `20260809130108_team_note_completion_rpc_and_assignment_notify` then applied on 2026-08-09 at 13:01 UTC from a concurrent session, lifting the live high-water above all five. `.claude/hooks/migration-ordering-lib.mjs` correctly refused them at that point: applying an older migration on top of a newer one is exactly the failure that silently reverted the `batch_apply_prepayments` actor guard on 2026-07-15 (row 857 is the repair for it). Each file was renamed forward with `git mv`, preserving relative order. The stale `20260808*` files were removed in the same commit so a clean from-scratch rebuild cannot apply the same change twice. This is the same remedy as row 808 -> live row 811.
>
> **The rename was NOT the only change — corrected 2026-08-09.** The re-issue itself added only a provenance header, but a follow-up RLS + drift review of the re-issued files then changed executable SQL in three of them. Do not skip the diff on the strength of "it's just a rename":
> - **Row 858 (`20260809170600`)** — the terminal `UPDATE order_items SET quantity_remaining = 0` is now gated on the delegated impl not having returned `already_cancelled`. Ungated, re-cancelling an already-cancelled order would have zeroed the historical remainders this migration explicitly promises not to touch (ORD-2026-0330 among them). Both reviewers found this independently.
> - **Rows 858, 860, 861** — each gained a closing `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role` on the function it emits. Verified live: postgres's default function ACL in schema `public` grants EXECUTE to anon, authenticated and service_role, so a bare `CREATE FUNCTION` on a fresh rebuild would hand anon EXECUTE on a SECURITY DEFINER order-cancelling function. On the normal path `CREATE OR REPLACE` preserves the existing owner-only ACL, so these are no-ops against current live.
>
> Row 859 (`20260809170700`) and row 857 (`20260809170500`) are unchanged apart from the provenance header. Each file's own header carries its exact delta list.
>
> **All five APPLIED LIVE 2026-08-09, 20:32-20:54 UTC**, one at a time, each behind its own freshly minted migration-apply-guard proof (both required Codex reviewers CLEAN) and a live post-apply read. Supabase assigned ledger versions `20260809203222`, `20260809204044`, `20260809204435`, `20260809204855`, `20260809205423` in file order; the version column above stays on the file timestamp, which is what the disk filename and the ordering guard both key on. The 49 pre-existing fractional-cent rows (46 `order_items.total_price` + 3 `commissions.commission_amount`, including the $5,245.195 pending payout) were counted before migration 4 and re-counted after migrations 4 and 5: still exactly 46 and 3. No live money moved. The repair statement at the bottom of `20260809170800` remains commented out and still needs its own explicit approval.
